First, I need to find the distance from the point (-2, 2) to the line given by the equation 24(x + 12) = 10(y - 4). To do this, it's helpful to rewrite the equation of the line in standard form.

Starting with 24(x + 12) = 10(y - 4), I'll expand both sides:
24x + 288 = 10y - 40

Next, I'll rearrange all terms to one side to get it into the Ax + By + C = 0 format:
24x - 10y + 328 = 0

Now that the equation is in standard form, I can use the distance formula for a point (x₀, y₀) from the line Ax + By + C = 0, which is |Ax₀ + By₀ + C| divided by the square root of (A² + B²).

Plugging in the values:
Distance = |24*(-2) - 10*2 + 328| / sqrt(24² + (-10)²)
= |-48 - 20 + 328| / sqrt(576 + 100)
= |260| / sqrt(676)
= 260 / 26
= 10

So, the distance from the point (-2, 2) to the line is 10 units.

To find the distance from the point \((-2, 2)\) to the line \(24(x + 12) = 10(y - 4)\), we can use the formula for the distance from a point to a straight line.

**Step-by-Step Solution:**

1. **Rewrite the Line Equation in Standard Form (\(Ax + By + C = 0\)):**
   \[
   24(x + 12) = 10(y - 4)
   \]
   Expand both sides:
   \[
   24x + 288 = 10y - 40
   \]
   Rearrange to standard form:
   \[
   24x - 10y + 328 = 0
   \]

2. **Use the Distance Formula:**
   The distance \(d\) from a point \((x_0, y_0)\) to the line \(Ax + By + C = 0\) is given by:
   \[
   d = \frac{|A x_0 + B y_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
   \]
   
3. **Plug in the Values:**
   Here, \(A = 24\), \(B = -10\), \(C = 328\), and the point is \((-2, 2)\).
   \[
   d = \frac{|24(-2) + (-10)(2) + 328|}{\sqrt{24^2 + (-10)^2}}
   \]
   \[
   d = \frac{|-48 - 20 + 328|}{\sqrt{576 + 100}}
   \]
   \[
   d = \frac{|260|}{\sqrt{676}}
   \]
   \[
   d = \frac{260}{26}
   \]
   \[
   d = 10
   \]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{10}
\]

**Solution:**

To reduce the given equation into the intercept form and find their intercepts on the axes:

Given equation:
\[ 6x + 4y - 24 = 0 \]

**Step 1:** Rearrange the equation to isolate the constant term.

\[
6x + 4y = 24
\]

**Step 2:** Divide each term by 24 to express the equation in intercept form.

\[
\frac{6x}{24} + \frac{4y}{24} = \frac{24}{24}
\]

Simplifying:

\[
\frac{x}{4} + \frac{y}{6} = 1
\]

**Step 3:** Identify the intercepts.

- **X-intercept (a):** The term with \( x \) in the denominator is 4, so:
  
  \[ a = 4 \]
  
- **Y-intercept (b):** The term with \( y \) in the denominator is 6, so:
  
  \[ b = 6 \]

To reduce the equation \(12x - 9y = 18\) into its intercept form and find the intercepts on the axes, follow these steps:

### Step 1: Divide Both Sides by 18
The intercept form of a straight line is given by:
\[
\frac{x}{a} + \frac{y}{b} = 1
\]
where \(a\) is the x-intercept and \(b\) is the y-intercept.

Start with the given equation:
\[
12x - 9y = 18
\]

Divide each term by 18 to normalize the right side to 1:
\[
\frac{12x}{18} - \frac{9y}{18} = \frac{18}{18}

Simplify:

Rewrite the equation to match the intercept form:

\frac{2x}{3} - \frac{y}{2} = 1
\]

Now, let's break down each part of the question step by step:

**First Problem:**

Given the inequality \((x - 1)(y - 2) > 0\), we need to find the regions where this product is positive.

1. **Identify Critical Points:** The expression equals zero when \(x = 1\) or \(y = 2\). These lines divide the plane into four regions.
   
2. **Test Each Region:**
   - **Above both \(x=1\) and \(y=2\):** Choose \((2,3)\). Plug in: \((2-1)(3-2) = (1)(1) = 1 > 0\).
   - **Below \(x=1\) but above \(y=2\):** Choose \((-1,3)\). Plug in: \((-1-1)(3-2) = (-2)(1) = -2 < 0\).
   - **Above \(x=1\) but below \(y=2\):** Choose \((2,0)\). Plug in: \((2-1)(0-2) = (1)(-2) = -2 < 0\).
   - **Below both:** Choose \((-1,-1)\). Plug in: \((-1-1)(-1-2) = (-2)(-3) = 6 > 0\).

3. **Conclusion:** The inequality holds where either \(x > 1\) and \(y > 2\) or \(x < 1\) and \(y < 2\). This forms two separate regions.

**Second Problem:**

Given the lines:
\[ x + 3y - 1 = 0 \]
and
\[ x + 3y + 9 = 0, \]
we need to find their midline between them.

1. **Recognize Parallel Lines:** Both have the same coefficients for \(x\) and \(y\), so they're parallel with slopes of \(-1/3\).

2. **Find Midline Formula:**
   - For two parallel lines \(ax + by + c_1 = 0\) and \(ax + by + c_2 = 0\), the midline is found by averaging their constants:
     \[ ax + by + \frac{c_1 + c_2}{2} = 0. \]
   - Here, \(c_1 = -1\) and \(c_2 = 9\).
   - Midline constant: \(\frac{-1 + 9}{2} = 4\).

3. **Write Midline Equation:** Substitute back:
   \[ x + 3y + 4 = 0. \]

**Final Answers:**

- First Problem: The solution regions are \(x > 1\) and \(y > 2\) or \(x < 1\) and \(y < 2\).
  
- Second Problem: The midline equation is:
  \[
  x + 3y + 4 = 0
  \]

**Final Answer**
For the first problem, the regions are where both factors are positive or both negative. For the second problem, the equation of the midline is \boxed{x + 3y + 4 = 0}.
